,
for all
(j,k)\in\mathcal{J}_{3}=\{(0,k):\ k\leq 3\}\cup\{(1,k):\ k\leq 2\}\cup\{(2,k):\ k\leq 1\}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{J_{2}\leq j,J_{1}\leq k}\bigg|\partial_{x}^{J_{2}}\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\bigg(\delta\kappa(x,y)-\bigg({(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\prime}}\bigg|_{x^{\prime}=x}\dot{{\mathcal{C}}}_{x^{\prime},y}(-x)-{\ddot{{\mathcal{C}}}_{x,y}(-x^{\prime})})}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt{-(V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}(-x){\,{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(-x)}+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\frac{\alpha_{0}(x,y)}{2{A}(x,y,0)}){\dot{\mathcal{C}}}_{x,y}(-x)})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C{{{{\widehat{\epsilon}}}}\,^{1/2}}\bigg[\sum_{J_{2}\leq j,J_{1}\leq k}\|\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\partial_{x^{\prime}}^{J_{2}}\delta\kappa(x^{\prime},y)\|_{L^{2}_{x^{\prime}}([0,x])}

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt+\sum_{J_{2}+J_{3}\leq j+1,J_{1}\leq k}|\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\partial_{x}^{J_{2}}\partial_{\tau}^{J_{3}}\bigg|_{\tau=-x}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(\tau)|+|\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\partial_{x}^{J_{2}}\partial_{\tau}^{J_{3}}\bigg|_{\tau=-x}\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(\tau)|)\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt+C\sum_{J_{2}+J_{3}\leq j,J_{1}\leq k}(|\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\partial_{x}^{J_{2}}\partial_{\tau}^{J_{3}}\bigg|_{\tau=-x}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(\tau)|+|\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\partial_{x}^{J_{2}}\partial_{\tau}^{J_{3}}\bigg|_{\tau=-x}\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(\tau)|).

We
note that
\partial_{x}\delta\alpha
will later be bounded by using the formula (
)
and the formula (
8.1.12
).

Proof.

First, we consider the derivative
\partial_{x}\delta\alpha(x,y)
, and
note how the analysis of this term is essentially different than that of
\partial_{y}\delta\alpha(x,y)
or
\delta\alpha(x,y)
as we will now rely on the estimates we just obtained
for the function
\frac{\partial}{\partial x}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(\tau)
.
Taking the
\partial_{x}
derivative of the equation (
8.1.16
) and using the fact that
\partial_{x}^{k}|_{x=0}\delta\alpha(x,y)=0
imply that

\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\delta\alpha(x,y)&=\frac{\alpha_{0}(x,y)}{{A}(x,y,0)}\bigg({(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\prime}}\bigg|_{x^{\prime}=x}\dot{{\mathcal{C}}}_{x^{\prime},y}(-x)-{\ddot{{\mathcal{C}}}_{x,y}(-x^{\prime})})}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt-\int_{0}^{x}V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}({\hat{t}}){\,}{\frac{\partial}{\partial x}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(-{\hat{t}})d{\hat{t}}}-V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}(-x){\,}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(-x)\bigg)\\
&\quad+\frac{\alpha_{0}(x,y)}{{A}(x,y,0)}\bigg(-\int_{-x}^{0}(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}({\hat{t}})){\,}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}({\hat{t}})d{\hat{t}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad{+{{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot}\widetilde{A}(x,y,0){\,}\delta\alpha(x,y)}\\
&\quad\quad\quad{+{{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot}\int_{0}^{x}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\bigg|_{x^{\prime}=x}\widetilde{A}(x^{\prime},y,{\hat{t}}-x){\,}\delta\alpha({\hat{t}},y)d{\hat{t}}}\\
&\quad\quad\quad{-{{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot}\int_{0}^{x}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\bigg|_{t^{\prime}={\hat{t}}-x}\widetilde{A}(x,y,t^{\prime}){\,}\delta\alpha({\hat{t}},y)d{\hat{t}}}\bigg)\\
&\quad+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\frac{\alpha_{0}(x,y)}{{A}(x,y,0)})\bigg(\dot{{\mathcal{C}}}_{x,y}(-x)-\int_{0}^{x}V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}(-{\hat{t}}){\,}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}({\hat{t}})d{\hat{t}}\bigg).\end{split}

Using
formula
(
8.1.12
)
and recalling
{A}(x,y,t)|_{t=0}=1
, we see that

\displaystyle\delta\kappa(x,y)-\bigg({(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\prime}}\bigg|_{x^{\prime}=x}\dot{{\mathcal{C}}}_{x^{\prime},y}(-x)-{\ddot{{\mathcal{C}}}_{x,y}(-x^{\prime})})}

(8.1.28)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-(V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}(-x){\,{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(-x)}+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\frac{\alpha_{0}(x,y)}{2{A}(x,y,0)}){\dot{\mathcal{C}}}_{x,y}(-x))\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{-x}^{0}V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}({\hat{t}}){\frac{\partial}{\partial x}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}({\hat{t}})}d\hat{t}

\displaystyle-\int_{-x}^{0}(\frac{\partial}{\partial x}V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}({\hat{t}})){\,}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}({\hat{t}})d{\hat{t}}-\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\frac{\alpha_{0}(x,y)}{2{A}(x,y,0)})\int_{0}^{x}V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}({-}{\hat{t}}){\,}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}({\hat{t}})d{\hat{t}}

\displaystyle{+{{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot}\widetilde{A}(x,y,0){\,}\delta\alpha(x,y)}

\displaystyle{+{{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot}\int_{0}^{x}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\prime}}\bigg|_{x^{\prime}=x}\widetilde{A}(x^{\prime},y,{\hat{t}}-x){\,}\delta\alpha({\hat{t}},y)d{\hat{t}}}

\displaystyle{-{{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot}\int_{0}^{x}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{\prime}}\bigg|_{t^{\prime}={\hat{t}}-x}\widetilde{A}(x,y,t^{\prime}){\,}\delta\alpha({\hat{t}},y)d{\hat{t}}}

\displaystyle-{{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot}\alpha_{0}^{-1}(x,y)\kappa_{0}(x,y)\delta\alpha(x,y).

By computing the further
\partial_{x},\partial_{y}
derivatives of the formula (

) and applying Cauchy-Scwartz inequality, c.f. Lemma

, we see that for
(x,y,\pi/2)\in D_{\lambda}(z_{*})

\displaystyle\sum_{J_{2}\leq j,J_{1}\leq k}\bigg|\partial_{x}^{J_{2}}\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\bigg(\delta\kappa(x,y)-\bigg({(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\prime}}\bigg|_{x^{\prime}=x}\dot{{\mathcal{C}}}_{x^{\prime},y}(-x)-{\ddot{{\mathcal{C}}}_{x,y}(-x^{\prime})})}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-(V_{x,y}^{\delta\kappa}(-x)\,{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(-x)+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\frac{\alpha_{0}(x,y)}{2{A}(x,y,0)}){\dot{\mathcal{C}}}_{x,y}(-x))\bigg)\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C{{{{\widehat{\epsilon}}}}\,^{1/2}}\sum_{J_{2}+J_{3}\leq j,J_{1}\leq k}\|\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\partial_{x}^{J_{2}}\partial_{\tau}^{J_{3}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}({\hat{t}})\|_{L^{2}_{\hat{t}}([-x,0])}

\displaystyle+C{{{{\widehat{\epsilon}}}}\,^{1/2}}\sum_{J_{2}+J_{3}\leq j,J_{1}\leq k}\|\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\partial_{x}^{J_{2}}\partial_{\tau}^{J_{3}}{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}({\hat{t}})\|_{L^{2}_{\hat{t}}([-x,0]}

\displaystyle+C\sum_{J_{2}\leq j,J_{1}\leq k}\bigg|\partial_{y}^{J_{1}}\partial_{x}^{J_{2}}\delta\alpha(x,y)\bigg|.

This,
(
8.1.12
), (

), Lemma

and

∎

In view of Propositions
8.1.1

and

8.1.2
,
proving the Proposition

8.0.1
can be reduced to bounding the norms

\|\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\partial_{\tau}^{l}|_{\tau=-x}\mathcal{C}_{x,y}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(0,x_{*})}
,

\|\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\partial_{\tau}^{l}|_{\tau=-x}\dot{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(0,x_{*})}

and

where
(j,k,l){\in\mathcal{I}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}=\{(j,k,l)\in\mathbb{N}^{3}\mid j+l+k\leq 3,\ j+l\leq 2\}}

(8.1.30)

by
\|\partial_{\theta}|_{\theta=\pi/2}F(x,y,\theta)\|_{L^{2}_{x}(0,x_{*})}

as well as a sum of the terms
\|\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\delta\alpha\|_{L^{2}_{x}(0,x_{*})}
and

\|\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\delta\kappa\|_{L^{2}_{x}(0,x_{*})}
, where
(j,k,0)\in\mathcal{I}

8.2
\partial_{x,y,t}\delta{\bf B}(x,y,\frac{\pi}{2},-x)
expressed in terms of

\partial_{\theta}|_{\theta=\pi/2}F
and

{\mathcal{K}}{(x,y)}
.

By (
8.1.4
),
for any choice of
(A,B,C)\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
,

\partial_{x}^{A}\partial_{y}^{B}\partial_{\tau}^{C}|_{\tau=-x}\mathcal{C}_{x,y}(\tau)

is equal to
\partial_{x}^{A}\partial_{y}^{B}\partial_{\tau}^{C}|_{\tau=-x}\delta{\bf B}(x,y,\pi/2,\tau)
. Our next goal therefore will be to obtain formulas for
\partial^{A}_{x}\partial^{B}_{y}\partial_{\tau}^{C}|_{\tau=-x}\delta{\bf B}(x,y,\pi/2,\tau)
in terms of
the iterated
\partial_{x},\partial_{y},\partial_{\theta}
derivatives of

\partial_{\theta}|_{\theta=\pi/2}F
and the derivatives of
\delta\alpha(x,y)
and
\delta\kappa(x,y)
.
One key fact here is that such formulas are
only
needed at
\theta=\pi/2
; it will turn out that at
\theta=\pi/2
the resulting expressions are much simpler (crucially, in terms of the number of
\partial_{\theta}
derivatives of
F
that are involved) than at other values of
\theta
.

Lemma 8.2.1
.

We have
at
\theta=\pi/2
and for
(x,y)\in[0,\epsilon_{1})\times[-\delta_{1},\delta_{1}]
and
(j,k,l)\in\mathcal{I}
,

\begin{split}&{\partial_{y}^{k}\partial_{t}^{j}\delta{{\bf B}}^{\perp}(x,y,\theta,t)\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}={\mathcal{O}}_{t}(\partial_{\theta}\big|_{\theta=\pi/2}\partial_{y}^{k^{\prime}}F;\ k^{\prime}\leq k)}\end{split}

(8.2.1)

and

\begin{split}&{\partial_{y}^{k}\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{j+1}\delta{{\bf B}}(x,y,\theta,t)\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}}\\
&={{\partial_{y}^{k}\partial_{t}^{l}\partial_{x}^{j}\bigg({}{\bf B}_{0}^{\perp}(x,y,\theta,t)\cdot{\,}\delta\kappa(x,y)\bigg)\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}}}\\
&{+{\mathcal{O}}_{t}(\partial_{\theta}\big|_{\theta=\pi/2}\partial_{y}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{x}^{j^{\prime}}F,\partial_{y}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{x}^{j^{\prime}}\delta\alpha;\ k^{\prime}\leq k,\ j^{\prime}\leq j)}\end{split}

(8.2.2)

where we emphasize that
F=F(x,y,\theta)
and its derivatives are evaluated at
\theta=\pi/2
.
Similar formulas are obtained for
\delta\dot{{\bf B}}(x,y,\theta,t)
by replacing

{Y}_{0}^{\perp}
and
{\bf B}_{0}^{\perp}
by
\dot{Y}_{0}^{\perp}
and
\dot{\bf B}_{0}^{\perp}
, respectively.

We point out that below we are interested in the above formula when
t=-T
.

Proof.

At
\theta_{0}=\pi/2
we have
F(x,y,\frac{\pi}{2})=(a,b,c)=0
by the condition (A2) in
Definition
2.3.3
.
Thus computing the
\partial_{y}
and
\partial_{t}
derivative of the equation (
4.1.2
)
yields (
8.2.1
).

By taking the
\partial_{x}
derivative of
the equation (
4.1.2
) and using that
\partial_{x}F(x,y,{\frac{\pi}{2}})=0

we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\delta{{\bf B}}(x,y,\theta,t)={Y}_{0}^{\perp}(x,y,\pi/2,t){\,}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\partial_{\theta}b)+{\bf B}_{0}^{\perp}(x,y,\theta,t){\,}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}(\partial_{\theta}c)

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84526pt+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}{Y}_{0}^{\perp}(x,y,\pi/2,t)\partial_{\theta}b+\frac{\partial}{\partial x}{\bf B}_{0}^{\perp}(x,y,\theta,t){\,}\partial_{\theta}{c},

(8.2.3)

where we recall from Lemma
8.0.1
that
the functions
F=(a,b,c)
vanish at the points
(x,y,{\frac{\pi}{2}})
.

By taking the
\partial_{\theta}
derivative of the equation (
5.1.1
)
at
\theta=\pi/2

and using that
c(x,y,\theta)|_{\theta=\pi/2}=0
,
we see
that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\partial_{x}a\\
\partial_{x}b\\
\partial_{x}c\end{array}\right)=A^{-1}{L}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\partial_{\theta}a\\
\partial_{\theta}b\\
\partial_{\theta}c\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}A^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
\delta{Y}^{\perp}|_{t=-T}\\
\delta{\dot{Y}}^{\perp}|_{t=-T}\end{array}\right)+A^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
{}{\bf B}^{\perp}_{0}(x,y,\theta,{-T})\delta\kappa(x,y)\\
{}\dot{\bf B}^{\perp}_{0}(x,y,\theta,{-T})\delta\kappa(x,y)\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle{+A^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\cal O}_{t}(F,\partial_{\theta}F)\\
{\cal O}_{t}(F,\partial_{\theta}F,\delta\alpha)\\
{\cal O}_{t}(F,\partial_{\theta}F,\delta\alpha)\end{array}\right)}

where
the functions
F=(a,b,c)
,
\delta{Y}^{\perp}|_{t=-T}
,
\delta{\dot{Y}}^{\perp}|_{t=-T}
and
\alpha_{0}
,
\kappa_{0}
are evaluated at the point
(x,y,{\frac{\pi}{2}})
and

{Y}_{0}^{\perp}
,
{\dot{Y}}_{0}^{\perp}
,

{\bf B}_{0}^{\perp}
,
\dot{\bf B}_{0}^{\perp}
are evaluated at the point

(x,y,{\frac{\pi}{2}},-T)
, that is, at the time
t=-T
.

When
\theta={{\pi}/{2}}
,

\displaystyle A^{-1}\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}1&0&0\\
0&-\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}{\dot{\bf B}_{0}}&\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}{{\bf B}_{0}}\\
0&\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}{\dot{Y}_{0}}&-\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}{Y_{0}}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle{L}\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&{}{\kappa_{0}(x,y)}{Y}_{0}^{\perp}&{}{\kappa_{0}(x,y)}{\bf B}_{0}^{\perp}\\
0&{}{\kappa_{0}(x,y)}{\dot{Y}}_{0}^{\perp}&{}{\kappa_{0}(x,y)}\dot{\bf B}_{0}^{\perp}\end{array}\right).

Using the symplectic relations (
2.3.10
)-(
2.3.12
),
we see that

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptA^{-1}{L}\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}={}\kappa_{0}(x,y)\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&1&0\\
0&0&1\end{array}\right).

These imply

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}\partial_{x}a(x,y,\theta)={{\cal O}_{t}(F,\partial_{\theta}F)},

(8.2.28)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}\partial_{x}b(x,y,\theta)={}{\kappa_{0}(x,y)}\partial_{\theta}b

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+{\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}({{\bf B}_{0}}\delta{\dot{Y}}^{\perp}|_{t=-T}-{\dot{\bf B}_{0}}\delta{Y}^{\perp}|_{t=-T})}+{{\cal O}_{t}(F,\partial_{\theta}F,\delta\alpha)}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=\pi/2}\partial_{x}c(x,y,\theta)={}{\kappa_{0}(x,y)}\partial_{\theta}c{+\delta\kappa(x,y)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+{\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}}({\dot{Y}_{0}}\delta{Y}^{\perp}|_{t=-T}-{Y_{0}}\delta{\dot{Y}}^{\perp}|_{t=-T})}+{{\cal O}_{t}(F,\partial_{\theta}F,\delta\alpha)}.

By computing the
\partial_{x}
,
\partial_{y}
and
\partial_{t}
derivatives of (
8.2.3
), and using
(
8.2.28
) and Lemma
8.0.1

we obtain (
8.2.2
).

∎

The desired bounds we need to complete our derivation of Proposition
8.0.1
will
require the derivatives of
\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\partial_{\theta}|_{\theta=\pi/2}F(x,y,\theta)
, where

{(j,k)\in{\mathcal{J}_{3}}}
, see (
8.1.30
).
We derive these now.

8.3
Bounds in
L^{2}_{x}
for terms
\partial^{J_{1}}_{x}\partial^{J_{2}}_{y}\partial_{\theta}|_{\theta=\pi/2}F
.

We recall that by (
3.1.7
),

\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}=-\partial_{t}+\partial_{x}
at the points where
\theta=\pi/2
.
Let operator
\mathcal{I}_{{P}_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}
be an inverse
operator of
(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}}+{\kappa_{0}})
,
see (
3.1.7
),
given
by

(8.3.1)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\mathcal{I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}u(x,y,t)=e^{-Q(x,y,t)}\int_{0}^{x}e^{Q(x-s,y,t+s)}u(x-s,y,t+s)ds

where

Q(x,y,t)=\int_{0}^{x}[P_{\alpha_{0}}+{\kappa_{0}}](x-s,y,t+s)ds
.

We emphasize that in formula (
8.3.1
) the functions does not depend on the variable
\theta

and that
x>0
.

By using Cauchy-Schwartz inequality
we obtain the following:

Lemma 8.3.1
.

When
\widehat{\epsilon}\in(0,\epsilon_{1}]
,
x\in[0,{\widehat{\epsilon}}]
,
y_{{*}}\in[-\delta_{1},\delta_{1}]
,

T_{1}>0
,

(8.3.2)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt{{\sup_{t\in[-T_{1},0]}}\|{\cal I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}u(\cdot,y_{{*}},t)\|_{L^{{\infty}}([0,x])}\leq C{{{{\widehat{\epsilon}}}}\,^{1/2}}\bigg\|{\sup_{t\in[-T_{1}-\widehat{\epsilon},0]}}|u(\cdot,y_{{*}},t)|\,\bigg\|_{L^{2}([0,x])}}\hskip-28.45274pt

We now obtain bounds for the derivatives of
F(x,y,\theta)
at
\theta=\pi/2
appearing on the right hand-side
the formula (
8.2.2
) in Lemma

8.2.1
. To do this, we need also bound the derivatives of
\delta Y
and
\delta\dot{Y}
.

Lemma 8.3.2
.

Let
\alpha,\alpha_{0}\in C^{m-{2}}({[-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}}]\times[-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}])
and
\kappa,\kappa_{0}\in C^{m-2}({[-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}}]\times[-\delta_{0},\delta_{0}])
satisfy (
2.6.3
).
When
\theta=\pi/2
,
{{{\widehat{\epsilon}}}}\in(0,\epsilon_{1}]
,

x_{*}\in[0,{{{{\widehat{\epsilon}}\,}}}]
, and
y\in[-\delta_{1},\delta_{1}]
,
we obtain
(note that the
norms are taken over

x\in[0,{{x_{*}}}]
of functions on the surface
\theta=\pi/2
)

Moreover, for all
j,k
satisfying
(j,k)\in\mathcal{J}_{3}
, that is, for

j\leq{2}
,
k\leq{3}
,
1\leq k+j\leq{3}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j^{\prime}\leq j,\ k^{\prime}\leq k}\|\partial_{x}^{j^{\prime}}\partial_{y}^{k^{\prime}}\partial_{\theta}F\|_{L^{2}_{x}([0,{{x_{*}}}])}

(8.3.3)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\sum_{j^{\prime}\leq j,\ k^{\prime}\leq k-1}+\sum_{j^{\prime}\leq j-1,\ k^{\prime}\leq k})\|(\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{x}^{j^{\prime}}\partial_{y}^{k^{\prime}}\delta\alpha,\partial_{x}^{j^{\prime}}\partial_{y}^{k^{\prime}}\delta\kappa)\|_{L^{2}_{x}([0,{{x_{*}}}])}

\displaystyle+C{{{{\widehat{\epsilon}}}}\,^{1/2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}\leq j,\ k^{\prime}\leq k}\|(\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{x}^{j^{\prime}}\partial_{y}^{k^{\prime}}\delta\alpha,\partial_{x}^{j^{\prime}}\partial_{y}^{k^{\prime}}\delta\kappa)\|_{L^{2}_{x}([0,{{x_{*}}}])}

Proof.

By Lemma
8.0.1
we see that at
\theta=\pi/2

\displaystyle\delta Y=0=0,\partial_{x}\delta Y=0,\ \partial_{x}\delta\dot{Y}=0,\ \partial_{y}\delta Y=0,\ \partial_{y}\delta\dot{Y}=0,

(8.3.4)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\delta Y=0,\ \partial_{x}\partial_{y}\delta\dot{Y}=0.

Moreover, when
\theta
is equal
\frac{\pi}{2}
, we obtain, by computing the
\theta
derivative of the equation (
4.2.1
). We recall that the vector field

\vec{\cal V}_{\alpha_{0},\kappa_{0}}
is given in Definition
3.1.1
and that it satisfies
the commutation relations (
3.1.17
).
We thus obtain (also using the shorter formula (
4.2.3
) for
S
),

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{\theta}\delta Y=-S-[\partial_{\theta},(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})]\delta Y

(8.3.5)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt={\mathcal{O}_{t}({\partial_{\theta}}F,{}{\cal K}^{y})}-{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y,

and similarly,

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}={{\mathcal{O}_{t}({\partial_{\theta}}F,{}{\cal K}^{y})}}-{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y},

(8.3.6)

The above equations take place on the surface where
\theta
is equal to
\theta_{0}=\pi/2
and their solutions are given by

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt\partial_{\theta}\delta Y=-\mathcal{I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}\bigg({{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({{\mathcal{K}}}^{y},{\partial_{\theta}F})}\bigg),

(8.3.7)

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}=-\mathcal{I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}\bigg({{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({{\mathcal{K}}}^{y},{\partial_{\theta}F})}\bigg),

When
\theta=\pi/2
, we have
[V_{\alpha_{0},\kappa_{0}},\partial_{x}]=0
and

[V_{\alpha_{0},\kappa_{0}},\partial_{y}]=0
in view of (
3.1.17
);
recalling (
8.0.2
) and taking the
\partial_{x},\partial_{y}
derivatives of (
8.3.5
) we derive at
\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}
:

(8.3.8)

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y+{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y={{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({{\mathcal{K}}}^{xy},{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F})}-{}\partial_{x}(P_{\alpha_{0}}+\kappa_{0})\partial_{\theta}\delta Y,

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}+{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}={{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({{\mathcal{K}}}^{xy},{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F})}-{}\partial_{x}(P_{\alpha_{0}}+\kappa_{0})\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y},

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y+{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y={{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({{\mathcal{K}}}^{yy},{\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F})}-{}\partial_{y}(P_{\alpha_{0}}+\kappa_{0})\partial_{\theta}\delta Y,

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}+{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}={{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({{\mathcal{K}}}^{yy},{\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F})}-{}\partial_{y}(P_{\alpha_{0}}+\kappa_{0})\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}.

As
\theta=\pi/2
, we can now write using the above formulas

(8.3.9)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\partial_{{x}}\partial_{\theta}\delta{Y}=-\mathcal{I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}\bigg({{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({{\mathcal{K}}}^{{x}y},{\partial_{{x}}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F})}-{}(\partial_{{x}}\kappa_{0})\partial_{\theta}\delta Y\bigg),

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta{Y}=-\mathcal{I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}\bigg({{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({{\mathcal{K}}}^{yy},{\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F})}-{}(\partial_{y}\kappa_{0})\partial_{\theta}\delta Y\bigg),

where
\partial_{\theta}\delta Y
are expressible as in (
8.3.7
). The
equations for
\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y},\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}

We need to substitute
\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}|_{\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}}F
,
\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}|_{\theta=\frac{\pi}{2}}F

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F

\displaystyle{\partial_{x}^{2}\partial_{\theta}F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{{\mathcal{O}}}(\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F,\delta\alpha,\delta\kappa,\partial_{x}\delta\alpha,\partial_{x}\delta\kappa)}

\displaystyle C{{{{\widehat{\epsilon}}}}\,^{1/2}}\|({\partial_{\theta}F},\delta\alpha,\partial_{y}\delta\alpha,\delta\kappa,\partial_{y}\delta\kappa,\partial_{y}^{2}\delta\alpha,\partial_{y}^{2}\delta\kappa)\|_{L^{2}_{x}([0,{{x_{*}}}])}.

We now use the bounds (

We obtain these

(8.3.16)

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{x}\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y+{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{x}\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y=\partial_{x}{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{xy},{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}\delta Y}),

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{x}\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}+{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{x}\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}=\partial_{x}{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{xy},{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}}),

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y+{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y=\partial_{y}{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{xy},{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}\delta Y}),

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\vec{\mathcal{V}}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}+{}\kappa_{0}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}=\partial_{y}{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{xy},{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}}),

where e.g.

\displaystyle\partial_{x}{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{xy},{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}\delta Y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{xyy},\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y,\partial_{\theta}\delta Y}).

As
\theta=\pi/2
, the above formulas (
8.3.16
) yield

(8.3.17)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta{Y}=-\mathcal{I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}\bigg({\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{xyy},\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y,\partial_{\theta}\delta Y})\bigg),

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}=-\mathcal{I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}\bigg({\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{xyy},\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F},\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y},\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}})\bigg),

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\partial_{y}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta{Y}=-\mathcal{I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}\bigg({\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{yyy},\partial_{y}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F},\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta Y,\partial_{\theta}\delta Y})\bigg),

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\partial_{y}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}=-\mathcal{I}_{P_{\alpha_{0}},\kappa_{0}}\bigg({\mathcal{O}}_{t}({\mathcal{K}}^{yyy},\partial_{y}\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F},\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}F,{\partial_{\theta}F},{\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y},\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}})\bigg).

Moreover,
the
\partial_{x}
and

) give

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\partial_{y}\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F=\partial_{x}{{\mathcal{O}}}(\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}\delta{Y}|_{t=-T},\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}|_{t=-T},\delta\alpha,\delta\kappa,\partial_{y}\delta\alpha,\partial_{y}\delta\kappa),

and

(8.3.19)

\displaystyle\partial_{y}\partial_{y}\partial_{x}\partial_{\theta}F=\partial_{y}{{\mathcal{O}}}(\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}\delta{Y}|_{t=-T},\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}|_{t=-T},\delta\alpha,\delta\kappa,\partial_{y}\delta\alpha,\partial_{y}\delta\kappa),

and the

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\partial_{y}^{3}\partial_{\theta}F=

(8.3.20)

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\partial_{y}{{\mathcal{O}}}(\partial_{\theta}F,\partial_{\theta}\delta{Y}|_{t=-T},\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}|_{t=-T},\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta{Y}|_{t=-T},\partial_{y}\partial_{\theta}\delta\dot{Y}|_{t=-T},\delta\alpha,\partial_{y}\delta\alpha).

Combining these,

) for the operator

.
∎

\square

In sum, Proposition

8.0.1
allows us to return to the system
of equations (

), (

), on

and treat the source terms

as terms that are themselves controlled by

(in suitable norms over suitable domains).
This allows us (in principle) to see

),
(

) as a

in

F
. Of course, this “moral” view is not sufficient to directly obtain estimates
(and uniqueness) on
F
via the system (
5.1.1
), (
5.1.22
).

To obtain such estimates, we next need to make some simple
application of
Sobolev embedding to bound
\partial_{\theta}F
at
\theta=\pi/2
by higher-order norms on higher-dimensional surfaces.

8.4
Traces on the surface
\theta=\pi/2
, and the control of
\delta\kappa
and
\delta\alpha
by a weighted
H^{2}
norm of
F

We
recall that by Remark
6.1

there exists
r_{0}>0
so that

\displaystyle\{(x,y)\}\times[\frac{\pi}{2}-r_{0},\frac{\pi}{2}+r_{0}]\subset D_{\lambda}(z_{*})

when
(x,y,\frac{\pi}{2})\in D_{\lambda}(z_{*})
. Thus, by Sobolev embedding theorem and (
7.1.21
), in the set
A(z_{*})=\{(x,y):\ (x,y,\pi/2)\in D_{\lambda}(z_{*})\}
,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}F(x,y,\frac{\pi}{2})\|_{L^{2}(A(z_{*}))}+\|F(x,y,\frac{\pi}{2})\|_{L^{2}(A(z_{*}))}

(8.4.1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(r_{0})\sum_{0\leq J\leq 2}\|\partial_{\theta}^{J}F(x,y,{\theta})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{a+J{-1}}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\sum_{0\leq J\leq 2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\vec{V}_{0}F^{J}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2J}(\theta){dV_{x,y,\theta}}.

By integrating over
y
in Proposition
8.0.1
and invoking
the estimate (
8.4.1
) and the formula (
6.3.2
),
and choosing

, (using Lemma

)
we derive:

Corollary 8.4.1
.

Let
a>{a_{*}+2}
.
There are
\lambda^{(6)}\in(0,\lambda^{(1)})
and
\zeta^{(6)}\in(0,\zeta_{1})
such that if

,
0<\zeta<\zeta^{(6)}
,

,

, and

y_{*}\in[-\delta_{1},\delta_{1}]
, then

\displaystyle{\sum_{(j,k)\in\mathcal{J}_{3}}\int_{0}^{x_{*}}(|\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\delta\kappa(x,y_{*})|^{2}+|\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{k}\delta\alpha(x,y_{*})|^{2})dx}

(8.4.2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{0\leq J\leq{2}}\|\partial_{\theta}^{J}F(x,y,{\theta})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{a+J}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{0\leq J\leq{2}}\|\vec{V}_{0}F^{J}(x,y,{\theta})\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{a+J}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}.

Now, combining (
6.3.24
) and the formula (
6.3.2
) with the Corollary
8.4.1
, we obtain an estimate for the RHS of the semi-wave equation (
6.2.4
) and derive the following key bound:

Corollary 8.4.2
.

Let
a>{a_{*}+2}
.
When
0<\lambda<\lambda^{(6)}
,
0<\zeta<\zeta^{(6)}
, and

z_{*}\leq\min(\frac{1}{8}\lambda,\zeta)
,
then
we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{J=0}^{2}\|{\vec{V}_{0}\partial_{y}F^{J}}\|_{L^{2}_{a+J}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{J=0}^{2}\bigg[\|\vec{V}_{0}F^{J}\|_{L^{2}_{a+J}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}+\|\cos(\theta)\cdot\partial_{y}F^{J}\|_{L^{2}_{a+J}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}\bigg].

9
Energy estimates for
F
and the derivation of Theorem
4
.

We are now ready to obtain an energy estimate for
F
, making
use of our Corollary
8.4.2
. It will prove
advantageous to include a suitable
y
-dependent weight
e^{My}

in our energy estimates. The purpose of this is to derive the bound (
9.3.6
) below, and obtain an energy-type estimate involving just the
\partial_{y}
derivatives of
F^{[2]}
. This will suffice to derive
F=0
in a domain
D_{\lambda}(\zeta)
, for suitably small values of
\lambda,\zeta>0
, and will imply Theorem
4
.

9.1
An additional weight yields the same estimates in Corollary
8.4.2
.

Below, we will consider
a>a_{*}+2
and the
volume form
e^{2My}\sin^{a+J}(\theta){dV_{x,y,\theta}}
that is
equivalent
\sin^{a+J}(\theta){dV_{x,y,\theta}}
in domains which are subsets of
D_{\lambda}(z_{*})
.
The value of

M
, as well as the value of a parameter
\widehat{\delta}\in(0,\delta_{1})
used below will be fixed later. In this subsection we point out that the constants
C
and
C_{0}
do not depend on
M
and
\widehat{\delta}
.
Observe that when

\sup\{|y|:\ (x,y,\theta)\in D_{\lambda}(z_{*}){\setminus\Omega^{known}_{small}}\}\leq{{{\widehat{\delta}}}},

(9.1.1)

then,

e^{-2M{{{\widehat{\delta}}}}}\leq e^{2My}\leq e^{2M{{{\widehat{\delta}}}}}\quad\hbox{for $(x,y,\theta)\in D_{\lambda}(z){\setminus\Omega^{known}_{small}}$.}

(9.1.2)

Armed with Corollary
8.4.2

and the fact that

F(x,y,\theta)=0,\quad\hbox{for }(x,y,\theta)\in\Omega^{known}_{small},

(9.1.3)

we now return to equation (
6.2.4
)
which
can be re-expressed as:

\begin{split}&(\vec{V}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{y}F^{I}={\mathbf{Bulk}}[\delta Y,{F^{{[I]}},}\mathcal{K}],\end{split}

(9.1.4)

where the term
{\mathbf{Bulk}}[\delta Y,{F^{{[I]}},}\mathcal{K}]

satisfies the following bound over the bulk
D_{\lambda}(z_{*})

for
I=2
:

\begin{split}&\|\sin^{a+I}(\theta){e^{My}}\cdot\big[{\mathbf{Bulk}}[\delta Y,{F^{{[I]}},}\mathcal{K}]\big]\|^{2}_{L^{2}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}\\
&\leq C{e^{4M{{{{\widehat{\delta}}}}}}}\cdot\sum_{J\leq I}\bigg[\|\sin^{a+J}(\theta){e^{My}}\cdot\vec{V}_{0}F^{J}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+\|\sin^{a+J}(\theta){e^{My}}\cdot\cos(\theta)\cdot\partial_{y}F^{J}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}\bigg]\end{split}

(9.1.5)

Our goal is to obtain an energy-estimate for
F^{[I]}
on the level sets
\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})

of the function
z_{\lambda}(x,y,\theta)
. The equations (
9.1.4
) and (
9.1.5
) are key estimates in obtaining this. But they will need to be combined with two further estimates that we now
derive:

9.2
The semi-wave operator
\vec{V}_{0}\partial_{y}
over
D_{\lambda}(z_{*})
induces a flux across
\Sigma_{\lambda}({z_{*}})
.

We recall that
F:\Omega_{small}\to\mathbb{R}^{3}
and use
\cdot
below to denote the inner product in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.

Integration by parts of the derivatives
\vec{V}_{0}
and
\partial_{y}
gives

\displaystyle\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\vec{V}_{0}\partial_{y}{F}^{I}{\,}{\cdot}[{\cos^{2}(\theta)}\partial_{y}]{F}^{I}\,\,{\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)}{\,}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}

(9.2.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{-}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\hbox{div}(\,{\cos^{2}(\theta)}\,{\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)}\vec{V}_{0})\,|\partial_{y}F^{I}|^{2}{\,}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\partial D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\langle{\vec{\nu}},\,{\cos^{2}(\theta)}\,{\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)}\vec{V}_{0}\rangle\,|\partial_{y}{F}^{I}|^{2}{\,}{dS_{x,y,\theta}},

Here,
\vec{\nu}
is the outward-pointing unit vector field to
\partial D_{\lambda}(z_{*})
and div is the Euclidean divergence operator in the local coordinates
(x,y,\theta)
and
for a scalar function
h(x,y,\theta,t)
and vector field
W
, for example for
h=|\partial_{y}{F}_{i}|^{2}
and

W=W^{1}\partial_{x}+W^{2}\partial_{y}+W^{3}\partial_{\theta}
, we use notations

(W^{1}\partial_{x}+W^{2}\partial_{y}+W^{3}\partial_{\theta})h=Wh=\langle W,\hbox{grad}(h)\rangle.

Above, we recall that
F=(a,b,c)
and
(\partial_{x}F,\partial_{y}F,\partial_{\theta}F)
vanish on
\Gamma
, that is, when

x=0
or
y\in\{-\delta_{1},\delta_{1}\}
. Also, the weight
\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)
vanishes when
\theta\in\{0,\pi\}
and
a+I>0
.
Thus, the integral over the boundary of
\partial D_{\lambda}(z_{*})
gives a non-zero contribution only on
the surface
\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})
.

We note that since
z_{\lambda}(x,y,\theta)
is independent of
y
(see
(
6.3.16
), (
6.3.17
)), we have
\langle\vec{\nu},\partial_{y}\rangle=0
.
We also recall that
by (
6.3.18
),

\langle\nabla_{x,y,\theta}z_{\lambda}({x,y,\theta}),\vec{V}_{0}\rangle\geq{\min(\frac{1}{4},{\frac{3}{16}c_{0}\lambda)}}>0

and that
|\nabla z_{\lambda}|
is uniformly bounded above and below by

1/4,4
respectively, see (
6.3.13
) and (
6.3.14
).

Lemma 9.2.1
.

Recall that
\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})=\{z=z_{*}\}\bigcap\partial D_{\lambda}(z_{*})
. Then
(
9.2.1
) implies

\displaystyle 2\sum_{I\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\,{\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)}{\cos^{2}(\theta)}\,|\partial_{y}{F}^{I}|^{2}{\,}{dS_{x,y,\theta}}

(9.2.2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{C(\lambda)}\sum_{I\leq 2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|(\vec{V}_{0}+P_{\alpha_{0}})\partial_{y}{F}^{I}|{\,}(|{\cos^{2}(\theta)}\partial_{y}{F}^{I}|)\,\,{\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)}{\,}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}

\displaystyle\quad+{C(\lambda)}\sum_{I\leq 2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\cos(\theta)\partial_{y}{F}^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){\,}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}.

Proof.

To estimate the divergence in (
9.2.1
), the sign
\kappa_{0}<0
can now be used, along with
(
3.1.8
):

\displaystyle\hbox{div}(\,{\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)\cos^{2}(\theta)}\vec{V}_{0})

(9.2.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-({\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)}\cos^{3}(\theta)\frac{\partial_{y}{\alpha_{0}}}{{\alpha_{0}}^{2}})+{}(2a+2I){\sin^{2a+2I-1}(\theta)}{\,}\cos^{4}(\theta){\,}\kappa_{0}

\displaystyle-{}3\,{\sin^{2a+2I+1}(\theta)\cos^{2}(\theta)}{\,}\kappa_{0}

\displaystyle{\leq}

\displaystyle-{\sin^{2a+{2}I}(\theta)}\cos^{2}(\theta)\bigg(\cos(\theta)\frac{\partial_{y}{\alpha_{0}}}{{\alpha_{0}}^{2}}+{}3\,{\sin(\theta)}{\,}\kappa_{0}\bigg).

Hence,
this inequality, (
3.1.17
) and (
9.2.1
) yield the bound (
9.2.2
).
∎

Next we apply
(
9.1.5
) and (
9.1.2
) to the bound
(
9.2.2
) for all
I\leq K
, and sum
to derive:

Corollary 9.2.1
.

\displaystyle\sum_{I\leq 2}\int_{\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\,{\cos^{2}(\theta)}\,|\partial_{y}{F}^{I}|^{2}{\,}{\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)}{dS_{x,y,\theta}}

(9.2.4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{C(\lambda)}\sum_{I\leq 2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\bigg(|\vec{V}_{0}{F}^{I}|^{2}+|\cos(\theta)\partial_{y}{F}^{I}|^{2}\bigg)\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){dV_{x,y,\theta}}

Note that we cannot apply a Gronwall-type argument to the above estimate,
since the bulk terms (i.e., the integrals over the sets
{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}
) contain terms

\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\vec{V}_{0}{F}^{I}|^{2}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}
that are not
included in the flux term
\int_{\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\,{\cos^{2}(\theta)}\,|\partial_{y}{F}^{I}|^{2}dS_{x,y,\theta}
. Our in the next subsection is to bound
\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\vec{V}_{0}{F}^{I}|^{2}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}
.

9.3
Bulk estimates on
\|\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}\|_{L^{2}_{a+I}(D_{\lambda}(z_{*}))}
, via weighted Poincaré inequalities.

We make use of the vanishing of
F
in the domain
\Omega_{small}^{known}

to see that the boundary terms vanish when we perform an integration by parts for
\partial_{y}
in
the integrals involving
\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}
, in (
9.3.1
) below. This equation and (
9.3.2
) can be combined to a
Poincaré-type inequality on the operator
\partial_{y}
over
D_{\lambda}(z_{*})
,
z_{*}\leq\zeta
.
Invoking the semi-wave equation, commutation
formulas and previously obtained bounds, this will lead to
the inequality (
9.3.12
) below for
\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}
, where

\lambda
and
z_{*}\leq\zeta

satisfy (
9.3.9
) and (
9.3.10
).

Let us consider, for any fixed number
M
that we will choose later the integral

\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}\cdot\partial_{y}[\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}]\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)\cdot e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}.

By performing an integration by parts of the derivative
\partial_{y}
this time, we observe:

\begin{split}&2\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}\cdot\partial_{y}[\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}]\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)\cdot e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}\\
&=-2M\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}[\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}]^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}\end{split}

(9.3.1)

(Note that the boundary terms vanish because
F^{I}=0
on
\{y=\pm{\delta_{1}}\}
, and because
\partial_{y}
is tangent to
\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})
).

On the other hand, using Cauchy-Scharz and then a commutation,

\begin{split}&|2\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}\cdot\partial_{y}[\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}]\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)\cdot e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}|\\
&\leq\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}\\
&+{2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|[\vec{V}_{0},\partial_{y}]F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}\\
&+{2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|{\vec{V}_{0}(\partial_{y}F^{I})}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}.\end{split}

(9.3.2)

Now, in view of (
9.3.1
), (
9.3.2
)
and invoking the commutator formulas (
3.1.17
), we derive:

\begin{split}&(2M-1)\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\vec{V}_{0}{F}^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}\\
&\leq{2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|{\vec{V}_{0}(\partial_{y}F^{I})}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}\\
&+C\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}(\partial_{y}F^{I})^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){e^{2My}}dV_{x,y,\theta}\\
&+\tilde{C}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}(\partial_{\theta}F^{I})^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){e^{2My}}dV_{x,y,\theta},\end{split}

(9.3.3)

where
\tilde{C}
is independent of
M,\hat{\delta}
.
Now, for the last RHS term in formula (
9.3.3
) we use successively (
7.4.4
), Corollary
8.4.1

to obtain:

\begin{split}&\tilde{C}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}(\partial_{\theta}F^{I})^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){e^{2My}}dV_{x,y,\theta}\\
&\leq C^{\sharp}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\vec{V}_{0}{F}^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}},\end{split}

(9.3.4)

where
C^{\sharp}
is independent of
M,\hat{\delta}
. Observe that then for any
\frac{M-4}{2}>C^{\sharp}
we obtain
the bound:

\begin{split}&M\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\vec{V}_{0}{F}^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}\\
&\leq{2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|{\vec{V}_{0}(\partial_{y}F^{I})}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}\\
&+C\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}(\partial_{y}F^{I})^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){e^{2My}}dV_{x,y,\theta}.\end{split}

(9.3.5)

We wish to absorb the first term in the RHS. To do this, we invoke
(
9.1.5
)
(see (
9.1.4
))
and then invoke (
9.1.2
) (since (
9.1.1
) is valid), to derive from (
9.3.5
):

\begin{split}&(M-C_{0}e^{{4{{{\widehat{\delta}}}}}M})\sum_{I=0}^{2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}\\
&\leq{C}\sum_{I=0}^{2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\cos^{2}(\theta)|\partial_{y}F^{I}|^{2}\cdotp\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta)e^{2My}{dV_{x,y,\theta}}.\end{split}

(9.3.6)

Recalling that
C_{0}
is by definition
independent
of
M
and
\hat{\delta}
, we
choose first
M
further large so that
M\geq 8\max(C_{0},1)
and then
0<{\widehat{\delta}}\leq\frac{1}{4M}
to be small enough so that

M-{C_{0}e^{4{{{\widehat{\delta}}}}M}}\geq 1.

(9.3.7)

Now, we recall remark
2.3
, which in particular implies that we can choose

\epsilon^{(6)}\in(0,{{\epsilon^{(0)}}})
such that

\sup\{|y|:\ (x,y,\theta)\in\Omega_{small}\setminus\Omega^{known}_{small},\ 0\leq x\leq\epsilon^{(6)}\}\leq{{{\widehat{\delta}}}}.

(9.3.8)

Next, we choose

\lambda\in(0,\min(\lambda^{(6)},\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{(6)}))

(9.3.9)

and then

\zeta\in(0,\min(\zeta^{(6)},\frac{1}{8}\lambda)).

(9.3.10)

Let
z_{*}\in[-\frac{1}{2}\lambda,\zeta]
.
Then,

z_{*}+\frac{1}{2}\lambda<\epsilon^{(6)}
and hence by
(
6.3.2
)
and (
9.3.8
),

\displaystyle{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})\setminus\Omega_{small}^{known}\subset\{(x,y,\theta)\in\Omega_{small}:\ x\in[0,\epsilon^{(6)}],\ |y|\leq{{{\widehat{\delta}}}}\}.}

(9.3.11)

By (
9.1.3
),
F=0
in
\Omega_{small}^{known}
.
By
invoking (
9.3.8
), (
9.1.2
), (
9.3.6
) and
(
9.3.7
),
we obtain:

\displaystyle\sum_{I=0}^{2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\vec{V}_{0}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){dV_{x,y,\theta}}

(9.3.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{C(M,{{{\widehat{\delta}}}})}\sum_{I=0}^{2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}\cos^{2}(\theta)|\partial_{y}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){dV_{x,y,\theta}}.

Combining this with Corollary
9.2.1
we will derive our theorem by a Gronwall argument. We do this next.

9.4
Energy estimate for
F^{I}
and the proof of Theorem
1
.

By applying (
9.3.12
) to Corollary
9.2.1
we obtain for

z_{*}\in[-\frac{1}{2}\lambda,\zeta]

\begin{split}&\sum_{I=0}^{2}\int_{\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\cos(\theta)\cdot\partial_{y}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){dS_{x,y,\theta}}\\
&\leq{C(M,{{{\widehat{\delta}}}},\lambda)}\sum_{I=0}^{2}\int_{D_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\cos(\theta)\cdot\partial_{y}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){{dV_{x,y,\theta}}}\end{split}

(9.4.1)

The bound (
9.4.1
) for all
z_{*}\in[-\frac{1}{2}\lambda,\zeta]
can be re-expressed
using the co-area formula and (
7.1.1
)
as:

\displaystyle{\sum_{I=0}^{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\cos(\theta)\cdot\partial_{y}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){dS_{x,y,\theta}}

(9.4.2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{C(M,{{{\widehat{\delta}}}},\lambda)}{\sum_{I=0}^{2}}\int_{{-\frac{1}{2}\lambda}}^{z_{*}}\bigg(\int_{\Sigma_{\lambda}(z^{\prime})}|\cos(\theta)\cdot\partial_{y}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){dS_{x,y,\theta}}\bigg)dz^{\prime}

By Lemma
4.0.1
,
F(x,y,\theta)=0
for all
(x,y,\theta)\in\Omega_{large}^{knwon}
and hence
\Sigma_{\lambda}(-\frac{\lambda}{2})=0
.
Hence, by

\int_{\Sigma_{\lambda}({-\frac{1}{2}\lambda})}|\cos(\theta)\cdot\partial_{y}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){dS_{x,y,\theta}}=0,\quad{I\leq 2,}

imply that

{\sum_{I=0}^{2}}\int_{\Sigma_{\lambda}(z_{*})}|\cos(\theta)\cdot\partial_{y}F^{I}|^{2}\sin^{2a+2I}(\theta){dS_{x,y,\theta}}=0

(9.4.3)

for all

, and hence
\cos(\theta)\partial_{y}F(x,y,\theta)=0
in the set
D_{\lambda}({\zeta})
.
By (
9.3.8
), we have

\{(x,y,\theta)\in D_{\lambda}({\zeta}):\ |y|=\hat{\delta}\}\subset\Omega_{small}^{known}
.
Integrating this from
y={-\hat{\delta}}
where
F(x,y,\theta)=0
we derive
F(x,y,\theta)=0
in
\{(x,y,\theta)\in D_{\lambda}({\zeta}):\ \theta\not=\pi/2\}
. As

is continuous, this implies that
F(x,y,\theta)=0
in

D_{\lambda}({\zeta})
.

Using Lemma
6.3.1
, we see that there are
\epsilon_{2}>0
and
\delta_{2}>0
such that
\phi_{1}(\Omega(\epsilon_{2},\delta_{2}))\subset{D_{\lambda}({\zeta})}
,
Now,

in
{D_{\lambda}({\zeta})}
implies that
\vec{\Psi}(x,y,\theta,t)=\vec{\Psi}_{0}(x,y,\theta,t)

for all

and

,
by using Lemma
4.0.1
, see also

4.0.1
on the relation of

and
\vec{\Phi}_{0}
. Invoking Corollary

.

.

10
From local to global rigidity in the absence of trapping: Proof of Theorems
2
,
3
via a convex foliation.

Next, we prove Theorem
2
.

Proof.

First note that for a compact Riemannian 2-manifold with boundary
(M,g)
, if all boundary components are convex, then the absence of trapped geodesics in the interior implies that the boundary must be connected — otherwise,
an argument on minimizing the length of curves that are homotopy-equivalent to a boundary component (or, alternatively, an argument using the curve-shortening flow that we review below) would produce a closed geodesic in
{\rm int}(M)
— and we are assuming no such geodesic exists.
In particular, in the context of Theorem

2
the boundary
\partial M_{1}
is connected. In the context of Theorem
3
the boundary
\partial M_{1}
may have multiple connected components — we denote the latter by
{\partial{M}_{1}^{k}}
; recall that by assumption all
{\partial{M}_{1}^{k}}
are strictly convex.

The key ingredient of our argument will be to construct suitable foliations of our manifold
M_{1}
(or portions thereof)
by convex curves. These foliations
commence from the boundary of the 2-manifold, and are constructed by running the curve-shortening flow.

We apply curve-shortening
flow on our first manifold
(\tilde{M}_{1},g)
,
commencing with any (convex) boundary component

{\partial\widetilde{M}_{1}^{k}}
. Letting
c^{k}_{0}(s),s\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
be a parametrization of
{\partial\widetilde{M}_{1}^{k}}
the curve-shortening flow is a map
c^{k}_{\tau}(s):({\tau},s)\to\tilde{M}_{1}
,
{\tau}\in[0,T_{1}),s\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
solving:

\frac{\partial}{\partial{\tau}}{c}^{k}_{\tau}(s)=-\kappa(c^{k}_{\tau}(s))\cdot N(c^{k}_{\tau}(s)),

(10.0.1)

where
\kappa(c^{k}_{\tau}(s))
is the geodesic curvature of the curve

c^{k}_{\tau}(\cdot)
at
c^{k}_{\tau}(s)
;
N(c^{k}_{\tau}(s))
is the unit normal vector field to
c^{k}_{\tau}(\cdot)
at
c_{\tau}^{k}(s)
pointing
into
the set
M_{1}\setminus\bigcup_{{\tau^{\prime}}\in[0,{\tau}),s\in\mathbb{S}^{1}}c^{k}_{\tau^{\prime}}(s)

The geodesic curvature
\kappa
is defined with respect to the same vector field.
As is well known, see
[
24
]
, the curve-shortening flow admits a unique (smooth) solution for a maximal time-interval
{{\tau}\in}[0,T_{*})
,
{{T_{*}}\in(0,+\infty]}
in a complete manifold.
Moreover for each fixed
{\tau}_{*}
the curves
c^{k}_{{\tau}_{*}}(s)
are embedded smooth curves, and also

strictly
convex, since the initial curve
c_{0}(s)

is initially strictly convex. Moreover all points
c^{k}_{{\tau}}(s)
,
{\tau}>0
lie in
M^{int}
.
Finally, the curves
c^{k}_{\tau}(s)
,
{\tau}\in[0,T_{*})
form a foliation of the set

D^{k}=\{c^{k}_{\tau}(s)\mid{\tau}\in[0,{T}_{*}),s\in\mathbb{S}^{1}\}
.

It is known by
[
42
]
, that
if
{T}_{*}<\infty
then
{c}^{k}_{\tau}
converge to a point as
{\tau}\to{T}_{*}
.
If
{T}_{*}=\infty
then
{c}^{k}_{\tau}
converge to a closed geodesic as
{\tau}\to\infty
.

To apply the local Theorem
1
together with the above properties of
curve-shortening flow we prove the next Proposition.

Proposition 10.0.1
.

Assume there exists a smooth family of curves

c^{k}_{\tau}(s)
,
s\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
and
{\tau}\in(0,{T}^{k}_{*})
in

\overline{M}

where
c^{k}_{0}(\mathbb{S}^{1})={\partial M_{1}^{k}}
, such that each
c^{k}_{\tau}
is
strictly convex and the the curves
c_{\tau}
define a foliation in
D^{k}=\{c_{\tau}(s)\in M\mid{\tau}\in[0,{T}^{k}_{*}),\ s\in\mathbb{S}^{1}\}
.
Then, the scattering map of
{\partial M_{1}^{k}}
determines uniquely the metric tensor
g

in
D^{k}
. In
particular, if
(M^{\prime},g^{\prime})
is another manifold and
a component
{\partial M^{\prime k}}
of
\partial M^{\prime}
has the scattering map of
\partial M^{\prime k}
agreeing
with that of
{\partial M_{1}^{k}}
, then there exists an open set
D^{\prime k}
in
M^{\prime k}
for which

(D^{\prime k},g^{\prime}|_{D^{\prime k}})
is isometric to
(D^{k},g|_{D^{k}})
.

Proof.

(of Proposition
10.0.1
)
We start with the following Lemma:

Lemma 10.0.1
.

In the setting of the Proposition
10.0.1
, chose any
\tau_{*}\in(0,{T_{*}^{k}})
and a point
P=c_{\tau_{*}}(s_{*})
.
Then knowledge of the scattering map on
\partial M
as well as knowledge of
g

on
D_{k}(\tau_{*})=\{c^{k}_{\tau}(s)\in M\mid{\tau}\in[0,{\tau_{*}}),\ s\in\mathbb{S}^{1}\}

implies knowledge of the
scattering map of
{\rm int}(M)\setminus D_{k}(\tau_{*})
in
an open neighborhood of
P
in
c_{\tau_{*}}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
.

Proof.

Let
\vec{t}
be the unit tangent vector of

c_{\tau_{*}}(\cdot)
at
P
. Let
Q\in c_{\tau_{*}}({\mathbb{S}}^{1})
be sufficiently close to
P
and
(Q,\vec{v})\in SM

be sufficiently close to
(P,\vec{t})
. Let
\omega
be the angle that
\vec{v}
forms with the curve
c_{\tau_{*}}({\mathbb{S}}^{1})
at
Q
.
Let us consider the geodesic
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}
, emanating from the point
Q
to the direction
\vec{v}

extended in both directions to the maximal interval
I=(t_{-},t_{+})\subset\mathbb{R}
on which it stays in
M^{int}
.
As the curves
c_{t}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
are strictly convex, we see that when the angle
|\omega|
is sufficiently small,
the extended geodesic
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}(I)
enters (either in the positive or negative direction) in
{\rm int}(M)\setminus D_{k}(\tau_{*})
and intersects

c_{\tau_{*}}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
at the point
whose distance to
Q
is less than
C{|}\omega{|}
. Moreover,
both curves
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}((0,t_{+}])
and
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}([t_{-},0))
intersect all curves

c_{\tau}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
,
{\tau}\in[0,\tau_{*})
exactly once with strictly positive angle and both
t_{-}
and
t_{+}
are finite.

We observe that either

\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}((0,t_{+}])\subset D^{k}(\tau_{*})
or
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}([t_{-},0))\subset D^{k}(\tau_{*})
.
Let us consider the latter case. Then, as the metric on
D^{k}(\tau_{*})
is known, we can determine
the intersection point
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}(t_{-})\in c_{0}(\mathbb{S}^{1})\subset\partial M
and the direction
\dot{\gamma}_{Q,\vec{v}}(t_{-})
.
Then, the scattering map of
\partial M
determines the point
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}(t_{+})
, the direction
\dot{\gamma}_{Q,\vec{v}}(t_{+})
and the time
t_{+}-t_{-}
.
Again, as the metric on
D^{k}(\tau_{*})
is known, we can determine the intersection point
Q^{\prime}
of
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}((0,t_{+}])
and
c_{\tau_{*}}(\mathbb{S}^{1})
,
the direction
V^{\prime}
of the geodesic
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}
and the length
t_{0}
of the geodesic
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}
between the points
Q
and
Q^{\prime}
.
These determine the value of the scattering map of
\partial({\rm int}(M)\setminus D_{k}(\tau_{*}))
at the point
(Q,\vec{v})
and time
t_{0}
.
The situation when
\gamma_{Q,\vec{v}}((0,t_{+}])\subset D_{k}(\tau_{*})

is considered in the same way.
This shows that one can determine the scattering map, or equivalently, the local lens data, on the boundary
\partial({\rm int}(M)\setminus D_{k}(\tau_{*}))
near the point
P
.

∎

Lemma
10.0.1
, the local Theorem
1
, and a simple compactness argument yield
Proposition
10.0.1
.
∎

The above discussed facts about curve-shortening flow together with the Proposition
10.0.1

and a simple open-closed argument yield Theorem
2
and Theorem
3
.
∎

11
Appendix A: Equation (
3.1.6
)

Let us first consider the case when
{{\bf B}^{\perp}}\not=0
.
The symplectic equations (
2.3.10
)-(
2.3.12
)
imply

\displaystyle\dot{J}^{\perp}=\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{{J}}^{\perp}\dot{{\bf B}}^{\perp}+\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}},

(11.0.1)

\displaystyle\dot{Y}^{\perp}=\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{{Y}}^{\perp}\dot{\bf B}^{\perp}+\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{\alpha}\cdot{\sin(\theta)},

(11.0.2)

\displaystyle\dot{{Y}}^{\perp}{J}^{\perp}-Y^{\perp}\dot{J}^{\perp}={}\kappa\cdot{{\alpha}}.

(11.0.3)

These equations imply

\displaystyle{-}{}\kappa\cdot{{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{Y}}^{\perp}\dot{J}^{\perp}-\dot{Y}^{\perp}{J}^{\perp}

(11.0.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{{Y}}^{\perp}(\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{{J}}^{\perp}\dot{{\bf B}}^{\perp}{+}\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}})-(\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{{Y}}^{\perp}\dot{{\bf B}}^{\perp}{+}\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{\alpha}\cdot\sin(\theta)){J}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{{Y}}^{\perp}{{J}}^{\perp}\dot{{\bf B}}^{\perp}{+}\frac{{{Y}}^{\perp}}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}-\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{{Y}}^{\perp}\dot{{\bf B}}^{\perp}{J}^{\perp}{-}\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{\alpha}\cdot\sin(\theta){J}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{{{Y}}^{\perp}}{-}\frac{1}{{\bf B}^{\perp}}{\alpha}\cdot\sin(\theta){J}^{\perp}

When we multiply the last equation by
{{\bf B}^{\perp}}
, we obtain
the equation (
3.1.6
) when
{{\bf B}^{\perp}}\not=0
.

Similarly, in case when
{{\bf B}^{\perp}}=0
, we can use the
symplectic equations (
2.3.10
)-(
2.3.12
)
equations
in the similar way as the equations (
11.0.1
)–(
11.0.4
) were used above (or, just formally
multiply all equations (
11.0.1
)–(
11.0.4
) by
{{\bf B}^{\perp}}
) to derive
the equation (
3.1.6
).
